### Category A: Memory access patterns (29 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `matvec-coalesce` | coalesced memory access | Strided read (uncoalesced) → transposed coalesced read |
| `coalesced-row-read` | coalesced memory access | Strided load (H*D apart) → contiguous load (adjacent) |
| `vectorized-pack-load` | vectorized memory access | Scalar load → 4-element packed load (16-byte transactions) |
| `grid-stride-loop` | work distribution | Fixed one-thread-per-element → grid-stride for arbitrary n |
| `gemm-tiling` | shared-memory tiling | Naive GEMM (global mem) → tiled GEMM (shared mem reuse) |
| `masked-boundary-load` | boundary-safe memory access | Unchecked load → masked load with fallback |
| `offload-cpu-bottleneck-to-gpu-kernel` | CPU→GPU data-prep offload | CPU transpose (674ms/step bottleneck) → GPU compute kernel writing into pooled memory (riir-gpu Issue 402) |
| `block-contiguous-weight-layout` | multi-buffer → single-buffer packing | 3 separate SoA weight buffers (pos+neg+scale) → 1 block-contiguous AoS buffer (1 access per group, riir-gpu gemm_ternary_block_contiguous, 3× fewer global-memory accesses) |
| `dequant-once-per-k-tile-into-shared-memory` | amortize dequant across shared tile | Dequant each weight element inline as consumed → dequant a weight tile ONCE into shared memory, let the hardware matrix unit consume it across all output rows that share the tile (riir-gpu gemm_ternary_simdgroup, m× amortization) |
| `hoist-group-invariant-scale-out-of-word-loop` | register-cached group invariant | Reload scale from buffer every word iteration → cache the per-group quant scale in a register across the 4 words it covers, reload only at the group boundary (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_block_contiguous, 4× fewer global loads) |
| `row-tile-amortize-input-load` | GEMV → small-GEMM via M-widening | 1 output row per plane (input loaded m×) → N output rows per plane with N register accumulators (input loaded once, reused across N dot products) (riir-gpu gemv_q4k_plane_rowtiled, Issue 609; gain scales with input-load/compute ratio: ~2× ternary, ~1.1-1.3× Q4_K) |
| `pin-lane-to-quant-sub-block-coalesce-byte-loads` | lane→element remap for byte coalescing | Strided lane map (each lane visits all 8 sub-blocks, 8 scattered loads) → lane-fixed map (each lane pinned to one sub-block, 8 bytes in 2 u32 words, 8→2 loads) (riir-gpu gemv_q4k_plane_factored, Issue 609 T2; also enables affine factoring by making scale/min loop-invariant) |
| `cooperative-block-wide-quantize-staging` | block-wide cooperative shared-memory staging | Separate quantize dispatch + global-memory read in consumer → ALL 256 threads cooperate to quantize disjoint chunks into shared memory once, then each warp reads from shared (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_cuda_raw `_fused` variant, Issue 616 T4; distinct from dequant-once-per-k-tile which is per-tile WEIGHT dequant, this is block-wide ACTIVATION quantize) |
| `cross-runtime-raw-handle-extraction` | cross-runtime raw native handle extraction | Host round-trip (GPU→CPU→native GPU→CPU→wrapper GPU, 4 transfers) → extract raw `id<MTLBuffer>` from wrapper handle via `as_hal::<Metal>().raw_handle()` and dispatch native kernel directly on wrapper-owned memory (0 transfers; riir-gpu gemm_ternary_metal_zero_copy, Issue 663 T5; generic for any wrapper-runtime→raw-native-dispatch integration) |
| `pool-buffer-aliasing-dedicated-output` | pool-buffer aliasing hazard workaround | Bind output handle directly (shares pool buffer with input → hazard tracking silently fails, kernel writes nothing) → write to dedicated buffer keyed by byte size, GPU→GPU copy back via blit encoder (1 copy, 0 host round-trips; riir-gpu gemm_ternary_metal_zero_copy, Issue 663 T5; distinct from inplace-output-alias Batch 19 which is parameter-count bypass) |
| `same-queue-implicit-ordering-no-sync` | same-queue implicit ordering | Separate queue for native dispatch (forces explicit `wait_until_completed`) → submit on the wrapper's command queue, rely on in-order execution within one queue (no host-side sync needed; eliminates the host-round-trip serialization that refuted Issue 656; distinct from chain-dispatches-single-sync-point Batch 19 which is intra-cmdbut) |
| `per-dispatch-pointer-re-extraction` | per-dispatch raw pointer re-extraction | Cache raw pointer across dispatches (use-after-free when pool defragments) → cache the wrapper handle (stable, ref-counted), re-extract raw pointer fresh on every dispatch (correctness invariant that makes cross-runtime-raw-handle-extraction safe; riir-gpu gemm_ternary_metal_zero_copy) |
| `dynamic-shared-memory-runtime-sized` | runtime-sized shared memory via extern __shared__ | Compile-time `__shared__ float smem[N]` (wrong when N depends on a runtime dim like head_dim) → `extern __shared__ float smem[]` with launch-time `shared_mem_bytes = head_dim * 4` (correct for any head_dim; fixes the Bonsai-27B head_dim=256 bug where CubeCL `Shared::<[f32]>::new_slice(128)` was too small; riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/attention.rs + deltanet.rs) |
| `ternary-bit-plane-u64-as-2x-u32` | ternary bit-plane sign extraction | Store ternary as i8 per element (3 bytes per 2 values) → two bit-planes (pos+neg sign bits) packed as u64 blocks cast to 2×u32 words, extract sign per column via `bit_pos = c%32, word_in_block = (c%64)/32` (64 ternary values per u64 block; riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/embedding.rs; distinct from base-3-trit-packing-5-per-byte which is arithmetic base-3 packing) |
| `combined-kv-buffer-keys-then-values` | combined KV buffer layout | Separate key + value buffers (2 bindings, 2 strides) → single contiguous buffer `[keys | values]` with shared stride, value at `kv[kv_half + p*kv_stride + d]` (1 binding, 1 stride; riir-gpu attention_cubecl/mod.rs L107-140) |
| `gqa-kv-head-sharing-bandwidth-reduction` | GQA KV head sharing | MHA (each query head owns a KV cache slice, n_kv_head == n_head) → share KV heads across query heads via `kv_group = head_idx * n_kv_head / n_head` (KV cache + bandwidth reduced by n_head/n_kv_head×; e.g. 2× for Gemma 2 2B's 8:4; riir-gpu attention_cubecl/mod.rs L150) |
| `widen-index-multiply-to-64bit` | integer address-width discipline | 32-bit index multiply wraps silently when position × stride exceeds 2^31 (long-context KV caches: t=500_000 × kv_stride=8192 = 4.1e9) → widen ONLY the address-producing multiply to 64-bit by casting one operand `(long)t * kv_stride`; keep loop counters + small dims 32-bit for register economy (riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/backward.rs L416/L474/L478/L515/L518 — every KV access; distinct from upcast-before-reduction which is fp32 precision) |
| `prebuilt-bind-groups-fixed-buffer-combos` | bind-group pre-build for fixed combos | A bind group binds BUFFERS, not data — dispatch sites whose buffer combo is fixed for the pipeline's lifetime (attention reads head_query + key_cache[layer] + value_cache[layer] + head_output; residual add reads hidden + residual + temp_out; each LoRA adapter has fixed input/output buffers) get their BGs built ONCE at init: [n_layer][n_head] attention BGs, [n_layer×6] adapter BGs, the fixed elementwise combos — the hot path only SETS pre-built handles (~768 `create_bind_group` calls per forward pass → 0; dynamic-buffer combos still build per call; riir-gpu forward/forward_buffers.rs L530-548 + L702-703, forward/mod.rs L412-501 P0-1) |
| `host-cache-immutable-device-weights` | host-side cache of device-resident constants | Constants on the GPU needed host-side every call (embedding tables wte/wpe, norm gammas, small biases) downloaded per call (2 full GPU sync stalls — the #1 measured forward bottleneck) → cloned to CPU ONCE at init; the host copy serves cheap gathers (token embedding = row slice + add, auto-vectorized) uploaded in ONE write (reverse test: any per-layer/per-token download of a weight-shaped buffer is this rule unapplied — e.g. the DeltaNet a_log/dt_bias downloads; riir-gpu forward/mod.rs L111-114 + L869-873 + L1270-1288) |
| `defer-readback-until-after-submit` | readback timing discipline | Downloading (map + read) GPU buffers while an encoder referencing them is under construction returns PRE-submission stale state — the recorded work hasn't executed, and any correction uploaded mid-construction is overwritten when the recorded pass finally runs; the readback's internal submit also breaks the in-flight encoder → RECORD the needed fixups (small correction list), finish + submit, then download FRESH data + apply + upload back (riir-gpu forward/mod.rs L291-295 + L1173-1183 — the SPEFT pending-corrections pattern, Plan 162 fix) |
| `persistent-zeros-source-encoder-copy-clear` | encoder-timeline accumulator clears | Re-zero accumulators via `queue.write_buffer` per layer (all clears flush before the whole encoder runs — layer 1 accumulates on top of layer 0's values) → keep a device buffer pre-zeroed ONCE at init (COPY_SRC, sized to the max clear target) and clear via `encoder.copy_buffer_to_buffer` from it: the copy lands exactly between the surrounding dispatches, zero host→device bandwidth per clear, no fill kernel needed (wgpu has none — the zeros source IS the fill; chunked fallback for larger targets; riir-gpu backward/mod.rs L235-241 + L267-268, backward/dispatch.rs L621-631 + L1106-1110, backward_dflash/mod.rs L309-310 + L618; three-way independent mining convergence) |
| `snapshot-transient-grad-for-deferred-download` | transient value pinning for readback | A host-needed gradient lives in a temp that later dispatches in the same encoder overwrite — downloading mid-encoder reads stale state, deferring loses the value → snapshot it at the correct dataflow point with an encoder-ordered copy into a persistent per-adapter buffer (lands exactly between the producing dispatch and the overwrite), then download after submit (the LIFETIME complement to defer-readback's TIMING rule; riir-gpu backward/dispatch.rs L885-897, backward/mod.rs L315-319) |
| `defer-readback-gated-by-live-output-size` | footprint gate on readback deferral | Deferring all sibling readbacks to one final drain is NOT unconditional — Bench 437 kept 5 concurrent ~70 KB FFN buffers alive and measured 16% SLOWER via Metal memory pressure, while 4 input projections (~8 KB total) chain freely with ONE sync instead of 4 → gate the deferral on total simultaneously-live output bytes; large footprints sync per stage and release each buffer (riir-gpu deltanet_input_proj_fused.rs L17-32 — the failure boundary the existing deferral rules lacked) |
| `weight-handle-cache-keyed-by-allocation-identity` | pointer-keyed device-handle cache | A GPU offload hook whose API must accept plain CPU weight refs keys a lock-free cache of pre-uploaded device handles by the CPU allocation's POINTER IDENTITY (pos_bits/neg_bits ptr pair, unique per layer): O(1) hot-path lookup, on-the-fly upload + insert on miss — the cache is invisible to the API surface and works with or without a preupload warm-up (riir-gpu deltanet_input_proj_fused.rs L279-281 + L301-327 + L361-372) |

### Category B: Compute patterns (34 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `autotune-warps-stages` | autotune dispatch | Hardcoded threads → sweep over (warps, stages) |
| `upcast-before-reduction` | numerical precision | fp16 reduction → fp32 upcast before mean/sum |
| `branchless-select` | branch divergence elimination | Conditional branch → select/if-expr |
| `unroll-fixed-inner` | loop unrolling for ILP | Dynamic loop → fixed-size unrolled |
| `forceinline-device-fn` | function inlining | Regular call → `#[inline(always)]` |
| `recursive-kronecker-cat` | structured matrix construction | Element-wise build → recursive cat (O(log d) steps) |
| `optional-bias-branch` | optional operand skip | Always-add bias → branch on `None` (skip when absent) |
| `shift-via-pad-subtract` | one-pass sequence shift | Two-pass shift+sub → fused pad+subtract view |
| `sorted-cumsum-threshold` | support-set selection | Iterative projection → sort+cumsum+threshold (single pass) |
| `polynomial-taylor-feature-map` | softmax kernel approximation | Full softmax → polynomial Taylor feature map (Based: 1+x+0.5x²) |
| `positive-feature-map-shift` | non-negative normalizer | Raw signed q/k → elu+1 shift (normalizer never crosses zero) |
| `hierarchical-log2-level-mask` | O(log seq) hierarchical mask | Dense N×N decay → O(N log N) sparse hierarchical (per-level scales) |
| `iterative-cg-solve-over-matvec` | conjugate-gradient solve | Materialize inverse → CG over matvec with Jacobi preconditioner |
| `chunked-test-time-gradient-descent` | mini-batch chunked GD | Sequential per-token GD → chunked GD with causal mask + last-step update |
| `expm1-for-near-zero-exp-minus-one` | numerical stability (cancellation) | `exp(x)−1` → `expm1(x)` (avoids catastrophic cancellation near 0) |
| `complex-mul-via-paired-reals` | complex-free representation | Complex dtype → interleaved `[real, imag]` pairs + 4 scalar muls |
| `stateful-shift-via-cat-prefix` | cross-batch stateful shift | Zero-pad shift → `cat(x_prev, x[:-1])` (carries real cross-batch state) |
| `squared-relu-activation` | sparse sharpening activation | `relu(x)` then square → fused `max(x,0)²` (zeros preserved, non-zeros sharpened) |
| `pre-rotate-once-exploit-orthogonality` | orthogonal pre-rotation identity | Rotate each key by R then dot with q → pre-rotate query once (`q'=R^T·q`) for orthogonal R, then dot `q'` with each unrotated key (riir-gpu spectralquant/attention, ~4096× fewer rotation FLOPs) |
| `cmma-hardware-unit-over-scalar-alu` | hardware matrix unit vs scalar ALU | Scalar dot in a plane (occupancy-bound, ~1.08×) → cooperative matrix multiply-accumulate hardware unit (tensor core / Metal simdgroup_matrix / cmma, 64 FMAs/cycle on the dedicated unit, riir-gpu gemm_ternary_simdgroup) |
| `skip-all-zero-packed-words-via-or-reduce` | ternary sparsity exploit | Always enter the 32-bit extraction loop → OR-reduce all tile rows' pos+neg bit-words, skip the loop when the whole tile's word is zero (riir-gpu gemm_ternary_batched) |
| `split-sign-accumulators-defer-subtraction` | deferred sign subtraction | Subtract pos−neg per bit inside the loop (32/word) → keep separate pos/neg accumulators, subtract once at the end (1/word + independent ILP chains, both ternary files) |
| `clamp-tile-indices-to-sentinel-discard-at-write` | divergence-free tile bounds | Per-lane bounds check inside the loop → alias OOB tile rows to m-1 sentinel, compute fully, discard redundant results at write time (riir-gpu gemm_ternary_batched) |
| `factor-affine-quant-scale-out-of-element-loop` | algebraic factoring of affine quant | Apply d·scale and dmin·min per element inside the dot loop → when dequant is affine in the code within a sub-block, factor scales out: accumulate Σ(code·x) and Σ(x) separately, apply scales once per sub-block (riir-gpu gemv_q4k_plane_factored, Issue 609 T2; Q4_K/min+scale-quant-specific — ternary is LINEAR in bits with no min offset) |
| `byte-perm-lut-via-permutation-immediate` | CUDA __byte_perm packed-alphabet decode | Shift+mask+sign chain per code (N codes = ~3N scalar ops) → __byte_perm(constant, constant, code >> shift) decodes 4 codes to 4 signed bytes in ONE PTX instruction; constant packs the signed alphabet as bytes (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_cuda_raw, Issue 608 T2; CUDA-specific sm_70+ intrinsic, no CubeCL/Metal analogue) |
| `dp4a-int8-hardware-dot-product` | NVIDIA __dp4a 4-lane int8 dot | Scalar 4-element int8 dot (~8 FMA ops) → NVIDIA __dp4a(a, b, c) computes a[0]*b[0]+a[1]*b[1]+a[2]*b[2]+a[3]*b[3]+c in ONE PTX instruction (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_cuda_raw, Issue 608 T2; distinct from cmma Batch 16 — that's CubeCL 8×8×8 matrix tile op, this is scalar 4-lane int8 dot, CUDA-only, requires int8 activations → ~6× kernel speedup over scalar ALU on ternary GEMV) |
| `threadgroup-subset-sum-lut-keyed-by-activation` | threadgroup subset-sum LUT (activation-keyed) | Per-bit shift+mask+select loop (~28 ops per 4-weight dot) → precompute 16-entry subset-sum table per 4-activation group in threadgroup memory, 4-weight dot = 2 reads + 1 subtract; ONE build serves EVERY row because the table is keyed by ACTIVATION subset (contains no weight/scale/zero-point) — structurally different amortization from the Q4_K LUT negative result (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_cubecl LUT path, Issue 606 T3c path 1, gated `ternary_lut_gemv`; Q4_K LUT was keyed by weight+scale+zero-point, rebuilt 160 tables/row → ate the fusion win) |
| `warp-shuffle-intrinsics-for-sub-warp-reduction` | warp-shuffle butterfly reduction for ≤32-thread blocks | Shared-memory tree reduction + syncthreads even for a 16-thread block (overkill — one warp) → `__shfl_down_sync(mask, val, delta)` butterfly reduction (4 shuffles, 0 smem, 0 syncthreads — hardware warp intrinsic; warp runs in lockstep SIMT). Companion: for the final 32-thread tail of a LARGER smem tree reduction, cast to `volatile float* vsmem` and skip `__syncthreads` — volatile defeats compiler register caching, lockstep makes the sync unnecessary (riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/mod.rs quantize_f32_to_i8 + attention.rs rmsnorm_batched final warp) |
| `softcap-tanh-attention-score` | bounded attention scores via softcap-tanh | Raw scaled dot product (unbounded — a single high score dominates softmax) → `softcap * tanh(raw / softcap)` bounded to [-softcap, +softcap], smooth gradient everywhere unlike clamp; `softcap == 0.0` disables for LLaMA/MiniCPM (riir-gpu attention_cubecl/mod.rs L188-191 + L373-376; Gemma 2 softcap=50.0) |
| `dual-accumulator-single-reduction-sweep` | dual-reduction barrier sharing | Two sequential reductions over the same elements (x loaded twice, two barrier trees) → one strided loop with two partial accumulators (x loaded once, feeding both) + both smem arrays reduced inside the SAME stride tree — halves global loads + halves the `__syncthreads()` count (riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/backward.rs L61-83; RMSNorm backward's sum(x²) + dot(grad_y·γ·x) is the canonical case; distinct from split-sign-accumulators which is ILP within one dot) |
| `monotone-transform-elision-under-argmax` | monotone-transform elision under order-only consumers | Apply softcap to the full logits vector before argmax (256K tanh evals/token) → a strictly monotone transform (cap·tanh(x/cap), clamps, positive rescalings) cannot reorder its input, so argmax/top-k consumers skip it entirely (the value-consuming CPU path still applies it; riir-gpu gemma2_cubecl/gpu_forward.rs L208-217; distinct from softcap-tanh-attention-score which APPLIES softcap in-kernel) |
| `tree-reduction-depth-over-sequential-accumulate` | summation-order depth for accuracy | Sequential-accumulate kernel (error chain depth = n = 2304, gradient divergence at 26-layer scale) → lane-strided + butterfly tree reduction (depth n/32 + log2(32) ≈ 77, ~30× better accuracy — Issue 424 fix; a perf-neutral kernel CHOICE changing only the summation order; distinct from upcast-before-reduction which raises input precision) |
| `softplus-piecewise-saturation-clamp` | far-tail softplus saturation | `ln(1+e^x)` evaluated directly overflows for large x (exp → inf) and wastes precision for very negative x → piecewise: x for x > 20 (softplus == x to f32 precision), 0 for x < -20, transcendental only in the safe band; thresholds pinned to the CPU reference so GPU/CPU stay bit-comparable — when the result feeds exp() decay on a PERSISTENT recurrent state, one inf poisons the state permanently (riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L863-874 + L970-980) |
| `fast-math-contraction-behind-feature-flag` | opt-in compiler-contraction gating | A numerics-affecting compiler flag is neither global (changes rounding everywhere, breaks bit-comparisons/tolerance gates) nor absent forever (the hot dot loop stays uncontracted mul+add on every build) → attach fast_math AllowContraction to the KERNEL ATTRIBUTE via a cfg_attr pair on a dedicated Cargo feature: strict-FP stays the byte-identical default, correctness gates run strict, perf builds opt in — the same source contracts to FMA only on the opt-in compilation (riir-gpu epilogue/gemv_residual_cubecl.rs L67-75 + L94-101 + L131-139) |

### Category C: Fusion patterns (22 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `fuse-norm-act-linear` | kernel fusion | 3-pass norm+act+linear → single fused pass |
| `fuse-quant-matmul` | quantization fusion | Separate quant+matmul → fused (BitNet b1.58) |
| `fuse-softmax-mask` | mask fusion in softmax | Separate mask+softmax → fused masked softmax |
| `fuse-xent-logsumexp` | cross-entropy fusion | Softmax+NLL → fused logsumexp (no prob matrix) |
| `fuse-add-rms-norm` | residual add + RMS norm fusion | 2-pass add+norm → fused (returns new residual too) |
| `fuse-group-norm-linear` | group norm + linear fusion | 2-pass norm+linear → fused (no intermediate tensor) |
| `fuse-jsd-logsumexp` | log-domain divergence fusion | Materialize prob matrices → logaddexp (no matrices) |
| `gather-selective-reduction` | selective reduction | Full softmax → gather selected tokens only (GRPO) |
| `fuse-gate-transform-chunk-cumsum` | gate + cumsum fusion | Separate gate + chunk cumsum → fused (no intermediate) |
| `rkv-receptance-correction` | fused reduce-then-broadcast | 4-pass triple-mul+reduce+broadcast-mul+add → fused `(r·k·ρ).sum(keepdim)·v + o` |
| `fold-norm-scalar-into-matmul` | norm-fold into matmul | Separate CPU norm pass + upload → fold inv_rms scalar into the GEMV dot product (riir-gpu gemv_q4k_rmsnorm, ~30s/step saved at 12B) |
| `fuse-gate-up-gemv-activation` | sibling-GEMV + activation fusion | 3 dispatches (gate GEMV + up GEMV + GeGLU) → 1 fused kernel (riir-gpu gemv_geglu_f16, saves 52 dispatches/decode at 26 layers) |
| `chain-dispatches-single-sync-point` | command-buffer-level dispatch fusion | N dispatches + N syncs → N dispatches + 1 sync (riir-gpu ternary_ffn_fused; linear dependency chains keep intermediates bounded; fan-out is the known-bad case per Bench 437) |
| `fuse-residual-via-in-place-output-array` | parameter-count bypass via in-place aliasing | Separate GEMV dispatch + ResidualAdd dispatch → single in-place kernel (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_residual_cubecl; output buffer doubles as residual input, bypasses CubeCL v0.10 5-array limit, collapses 128→64 dispatches/token) |
| `fused-activation-quantize-for-gemv-consumer` | activation + absmax + int8 quantize fusion | Separate activation kernel (writes f32 to HBM) + quantize kernel (reads f32, writes i8) → fused single kernel (activation + per-block absmax warp-shuffle + int8 quantize, one HBM write of i8; eliminates intermediate f32 round-trip ~2.6 MB/token for RMSNorm, ~115 KB/layer for SwiGLU; riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/mod.rs Issues 623/625/626/627 — rmsnorm_quantize, swiglu_quantize, gate_silu/sigmoid_quantize, rmsnorm_gate_silu_quantize three-way fusion) |
| `dual-grad-same-pass` | backward fan-out: both partials in one pass | A product-rule activation out = f(gate)·g(other) has TWO partial derivatives sharing the same inputs + the same sigmoid evaluation → emit both from ONE kernel (load each element once, evaluate activation + derivative once, write both grad buffers; halves input reads + transcendental evals + launches; riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/backward.rs L108-126/L137-155/L169-186/L345-369 — SwiGLU/z-gating/output-gate/beta-decay backward; distinct from fused-backward-chain (chain) + fuse-gate-up-gemv-activation (forward siblings)) |
| `fan-out-shared-input-single-sync` | sibling-dispatch input hoisting + batched sync | N self-contained dispatch helpers (each re-uploads the shared input + syncs per read: N uploads + N syncs) → create the input handle ONCE, clone it into each launch, queue all N, read outputs back-to-back — the first read executes the whole batched submission (1 upload + 1 effective sync; riir-gpu gemma2_cubecl/dispatch.rs L69-122 `dispatch_qkv`; NOT the Bench 437 known-bad DAG fan-out — outputs here are independent and read once; distinct from chain-dispatches-single-sync-point (dependent) + fuse-gate-up-gemv-activation (single-kernel fusion)) |
| `fuse-sibling-target-grads-single-kernel` | sibling-target backward fusion | N sibling targets (Q/K/V) each running the same two backward kernels (2×N dispatches + N−1 combine adds) → fuse per KERNEL KIND, not per target: one dispatch computes the summed input-grad in-kernel (d_input = Σ Rᵢᵀ·gᵢ — one output write, no combine pass) + one dispatch computes all N param grads in a single grid (N × blocks × per_block); all adapters + the SHARED saved input (all siblings saved the same input) in ONE wide bind group (6 → 2; riir-gpu backward/dispatch.rs L678-685 + L707 + L849-851; distinct from dual-grad-same-pass (partials of ONE op) + fan-out (forward 1→N mirror)) |
| `fused-conv-state-shift-dot-silu` | stateful conv producer fusion | Three passes with two intermediate buffers (shift window, convolve, activate) → ONE kernel: each channel's thread shifts the sliding window IN PLACE, computes the depthwise dot from the freshly shifted state, applies SiLU, writes back over the input — zero intermediates, window state persists in global memory across decode steps (riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L398-448; the batched/prefill form CANNOT alias in-place — see nonlinearity-forces-raw-input-buffers) |
| `epilogue-at-register-write-phase` | elementwise fusion at the producer's store | GEMV writes its output vector to global, then a second elementwise kernel reads it back + rewrites (one extra dispatch + a full round-trip per use site) → in a plane-cooperative GEMV, plane_sum leaves the full row dot in every lane's REGISTERS, so the epilogue (read residual[row], add) rides lane 0's existing single store: one extra scalar read amortized over the n-length cooperative dot, 2→1 dispatches (52/26 layers, 104 combined with norm+residual; riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L8-26 + gemv_residual_cubecl.rs L103-109 — the CODA insight) |
| `delay-rms-scale-past-gemv-epilogue` | norm-scalar commutation past a GEMV | RMSNorm runs as its own kernel BEFORE the GEMV (write the normed vector, GEMV reads it back) → the per-row scalar r = 1/sqrt(mean(x²)+eps) COMMUTES with the matmul: extract r (partial norm), apply only γ elementwise, land r in the downstream GEMV's row-scale epilogue (composable with residual); the honest accounting: pure reparam saves ZERO dispatches (3→3) — the win is last-GEMV fusion + the eliminated normed-vector write, and the dominant benefit is dispatch/command-buffer overhead, NOT bandwidth (riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L118-133 + L227-262, coda_primitives_cubecl.rs L20-24) |
| `fuse-count-into-mutation-pass` | count fused into its producing mutation | A mutation pass writes a predicate-derived output (a mask), then a SECOND pass scans the output to count affected elements → accumulate the count INSIDE the mutation loop — increments ride already-hot cache lines, the second O(n) scan (or second reduction kernel) disappears; the count feeds loss normalization + the empty-workset gate (in kernel form the predicate write and its reduction share one launch; riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L828-847 + L585-596) |

### Category D: Dispatch patterns (31 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `sm-count-block-size` | occupancy-aware block sizing | Fixed block → SM-aware `min(128, next_pow2)` |
| `predicate-gated-backend` | conditional kernel dispatch | Unconditional fast path → shape/dtype predicate gate |
| `pow2-block-alignment` | power-of-2 alignment | Arbitrary dim → `next_power_of_two` |
| `predicate-gate-div512` | shape-divisibility gate | Unconditional packed path → `inner_dim % 512 == 0` gate |
| `multi-condition-predicate` | compound shape+dtype gate | Single condition → multi-condition (rows%32 AND width%8) |
| `layer-depth-decay-schedule` | depth-aware parameter schedule | Constant decay slope → slope ∝ (1 − layer_idx/num_layers) |
| `dual-mode-gate-transform` | dual-mode gate dispatch | Hardcoded formula → unbounded softplus / bounded sigmoid by parameter |
| `cyclic-modality-table-selection` | multimodal RoPE dispatch | Single RoPE table → split head_dim into sections, cyclic gather from N modality tables |
| `swap-large-axis-to-x-for-workgroup-distribution` | dispatch axis swap for SM distribution | (M,P) cube count with small M on X under-utilizes SMs → (P,M) swap so the large axis gets X-tiles for even workgroup distribution (riir-gpu gemm_ternary_simdgroup, mirrors matmul_swap_ab) |
| `no-blocking-sync-in-async-pipeline` | no blocking sync in async pipeline | `wait_until_completed` after every dispatch (serializes the async pipeline, 448 blocking round-trips per prefill) → submit and return; rely on queue ordering + caller's pre-dispatch sync for correctness (only non-queued test reads need explicit sync; riir-gpu gemm_ternary_metal_zero_copy, Issue 663 T5; complement of chain-dispatches-single-sync-point Batch 19 which reduces N syncs to 1, this eliminates the final sync) |
| `cuda-graph-scalar-via-device-pointer` | CUDA graph capture via device-pointer scalar | Scalar arg baked into captured CUDA graph (replays same value forever) → ship a `_devpos` variant that reads the scalar from a `const int*` device pointer dereferenced at kernel runtime; caller writes new value via `memcpy_htod` before `graph.launch()` so the captured graph processes different inputs each call (riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/embedding.rs + attention.rs, Issue 618; 4 variants: dequant_wte_row, rope_partial, kv_cache_append, attention_decode) |
| `fold-query-tokens-for-occupancy` | query-token folding for SM occupancy | One workgroup per head (n_head < SM_count → idle SMs during speculative decode) → fold multiple query tokens into one workgroup via `fold_factor()`: largest divisor of `seq_len_q` ≤ `workgroup_size / n_head`; kernel loops `for q_tok in 0..fold` inside each workgroup (riir-gpu attention_cubecl/mod.rs Plan 179 D1; gated `fold_dispatch`; e.g. Gemma 2 2B 8 heads × fold=4 = 32 token-heads vs 8) |
| `hardcoded-fast-path-jit-specialization` | hardcoded fast-path for JIT constant-folding | Parametric kernel reads dims from buffer (JIT cannot constant-fold — loop bounds unknown) → when params match a known config, dispatch a hardcoded variant baking constants into the kernel body; JIT constant-folds into optimized Metal/WGSL shaders (unrolled loops, known strides; ~24% decode throughput 31.4→41.4 tok/s on M3 Max; riir-gpu attention_cubecl/mod.rs L1313-1342; distinct from autotune-warps-stages which searches configs — here the config is matched) |
| `device-side-argmax-minimal-download` | boundary-crossing shrink via device-side reduction | Download full logits (~1 MB/token at 256K vocab) for CPU argmax → dedicated GPU argmax reduction kernel downloads only the winning index (4 bytes; ~250,000× smaller per-token crossing; a 50-prompt + 500-decode session goes ~550 MB → ~50 MB — prefill still reads full logits once, decode never does; riir-gpu gemma2_cubecl/gpu_forward.rs L319-346 + L348-423; distinct from offload-cpu-bottleneck — the CPU isn't slow, the download feeding it is the cost) |
| `hot-swap-weights-preserve-autotune-caches` | weight hot-swap preserving runtime caches | Rebuild the whole GPU model per training step (re-runs autotune benchmarking + JIT compilation, ~seconds each) → `replace_weights` swaps only the weight handles + CPU copies, keeping the client + autotune cache + JIT kernel cache + tables + layer params (per-step cost = buffer uploads only; riir-gpu gemma4_cubecl/mod.rs L215-235, the LoRA training loop) |
| `per-slot-uniform-buffer-write-ordering` | uniform-write channel vs the encoder | wgpu flushes ALL `queue.write_buffer` calls BEFORE the recorded encoder executes — N dispatches in one encoder sharing ONE uniform buffer all read the LAST write (per-head attention params, per-target LoRA params silently corrupted; Issues 366/367/368 lineage) → one small (64-byte) uniform buffer per dispatch SLOT (per head / per adapter / per QKV target / per batched matmul slot), each wired into its own pre-built bind group, so concurrent writes in one encoder cannot collide (riir-gpu forward/mod.rs L141-148 + L361-390, forward_buffers.rs L550-557; distinct from same-queue-implicit-ordering — that is dispatch ordering, this is the uniform channel racing the encoder) |
| `derive-params-from-global-invocation-id` | grid-encoded per-item parameters | Per-item parameter (query position, head index) written to a uniform from the host — which under the write_buffer ordering hazard forces an encoder + submit PER ITEM — → encode the parameter in the dispatch grid: flatten (head, item) into workgroup IDs, derive in-kernel from `global_invocation_id` (one dispatch of n_head × seq_len workgroups with causal masking + GQA mapping computed inside; zero per-position uniform writes, zero per-position submits; riir-gpu forward/batched_dispatch.rs L280-350, dispatch.rs L316-321) |
| `merge-dispatches-into-single-compute-pass` | pass-level dispatch merging | One compute pass per dispatch (pass begin/end boundary cost × N) → dependent dispatches queued in ONE compute pass (set_pipeline/set_bind_group/dispatch × N inside a single begin/end) execute IN ORDER with memory ordering — the consumer reads the producer's writes without a separate pass (6 LoRA dispatches in one pass; pure input-save copies ride the copy channel, `encoder.copy_buffer_to_buffer`, outside any pass; riir-gpu forward/adapter_merge.rs L410-424 + L654-770; distinct from chain-dispatches-single-sync-point which is command-buffer-level sync amortization) |
| `batched-downloads-single-sync-cycle` | readback batching into one sync cycle | One encoder + submit + map + poll per download (sync × N; ~1.28 ms per measured sync regardless of data size) → N readbacks share ONE command encoder, ONE submit, ONE `map_async`, ONE `device.poll(Wait)`: pack each request into a contiguous non-overlapping 4-byte-aligned region of a single grow-only staging buffer, map the whole range once, slice per request (the 4-buffer LoRA-Muon step saves ~3.8 ms/step; riir-gpu buffer.rs L263-280, Issue 421 P0.5) |
| `chunk-encoder-submits-below-driver-limit` | driver-forced submission granularity | One encoder for the whole sequence (~24 compute passes/position → thousands of passes; Metal DEADLOCKS past the compute-pass-per-encoder ceiling, and accumulating write_buffer + command buffers before submit stalls the driver) → chunk recorded work (~48 passes/encoder at chunk_size 2), submit each chunk IMMEDIATELY, env-tunable chunk size (tiny-model training loops shrink it, long-prefill raises it; riir-gpu forward/mod.rs L1403-1411 + L1456-1458, Issue 016) |
| `staging-packed-uniforms-encoder-ordered-copies` | single-upload uniform distribution | N per-dispatch `queue.write_buffer` calls on a shared uniform (all flush pre-encoder — last-write-wins corruption + N small uploads) → pack ALL uniform params for the whole pass into ONE 16-byte-aligned staging vec, upload once, then move each dispatch's slice via `encoder.copy_buffer_to_buffer` recorded immediately before the consuming dispatch — the encoder timeline lands each copy exactly between dispatches, so ONE shared uniform can change value per dispatch (the 1-buffer complement to per-slot's N buffers; riir-gpu backward/mod.rs L15 + L1293-1295 + L1505-1512, backward/staging.rs L31-38, backward_dflash/mod.rs L774-780 + L944 + L980-986) |
| `identical-uniforms-share-single-slot` | idempotent-write uniform sharing | N per-slot uniform buffers + bind groups allocated even though every site writes byte-identical params → sharing is safe IFF every write is byte-identical (the last write is idempotent with all earlier ones — flush order is moot): share ONE uniform + ONE bind group, push the value once in staging + reuse the byte offset; the safety contract lives at the API boundary ("caller must provide a unique uniform if the count differs across calls in the same encoder"; riir-gpu backward/mod.rs L192-196 + L1447-1452, backward/dispatch.rs L315-320) |
| `per-layer-strided-grad-destination` | consumer-selected write destinations | Accumulate a gradient into one shared buffer, then run a post-hoc de-interleave pass per layer for a per-layer consumer → write directly to layer-major strided offsets (layer × seq × n + pos × n) in one pre-partitioned buffer, selected at dispatch time — the SAME dispatches produce the per-layer split for free (no second pass, no scatter), downstream reads contiguous layer slices, runtime-switchable destination (riir-gpu backward_dflash/mod.rs L205-207 + L1533-1541) |
| `token-flattened-elementwise-modulo-shared-weights` | mixed-arity launch flattening | Per-token elementwise launch inside a prefill loop (P × layers = thousands of dispatches, ~25 CPU allocations + launch latency each) → flatten [p, n_head] into ONE 1-D index space: per-token buffers index flat (i), per-head weight tables index i % n_head (weights repeat every n_head entries); bit-identical by independence; fixed 256-thread workgroups avoid the single-token form's workgroup ceiling (riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L922-993, Issue 637 T5/638) |
| `split-decay-outputs-eliminate-readback` | consumer-shaped output split | Compact packing of heterogeneous outputs into one buffer (total at index C of [H, C+1]) forces a device→host→device readback-unpack-reupload when consumers read [H, C] and [H] — and direct reads hit the WRONG offset (total_decay of head h-1) → write each component directly into the buffer shape its consumer reads: one kernel, two output buffers, the host round trip disappears (riir-gpu deltanet_chunked_cubecl.rs L342-349; the eliminated hack documented at L1949-1969) |
| `nonlinearity-forces-raw-input-buffers` | in-place aliasing's batching boundary | A sequential kernel's in-place trick (activated output written back over its own input) is INCOMPATIBLE with batched/widened execution when a nonlinearity sits between producer and windowed consumer: later tokens' windows read earlier tokens' POST-activation values — silently wrong, same dtype, no error → separate read-only RAW inputs + write-only outputs; the inter-chunk carry holds RAW values too (riir-gpu deltanet_chunked_cubecl.rs L27-47; the negative space of inplace-output-alias) |
| `first-readback-pays-queue-drain` | burst-ordered readback cost model | 'N read calls = N GPU syncs' is false after one dispatch burst on one queue: the FIRST blocking read drains the whole queue and every later read of an already-completed buffer returns immediately — N sequential readbacks cost ~1 sync + N small transfers; no per-read defensive syncs, and batched-download machinery is unnecessary when reads naturally follow one burst (syncs are a function of BURSTS, not read CALLS; riir-gpu deltanet_input_proj_fused.rs L242-245) |
| `pack-scalar-params-into-combined-buffer` | kernel-arg-budget scalar packing | When the kernel DSL caps Array parameters (~4-5 in CubeCL v0.10), per-scalar args + per-thread derived recompute blow the budget → pack ALL launch scalars (eps, dim, derived constants) into ONE small f32 params buffer created per launch on the host (inv_dim = 1/dim computed once, not per-thread), index positionally in-kernel, bitcast u32s through the f32 pack when scalar args run out, and couple variant choice to the budget via total_array_count()-style metadata (riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L48 + L148-154, coda_primitives_cubecl.rs L51-55 + L144-146, norm_residual_cubecl.rs L179-181 + L351-354) |
| `launch-unchecked-caller-owned-size-contract` | elide per-launch validation | The checked launch path re-validates buffer sizes/arg consistency EVERY dispatch — host overhead dominating a 14×26 loop of microsecond dispatches → compile with launch_unchecked, wrap in a launcher taking exact element counts as plain args, push the size contract into a documented # Safety section (per-handle element counts, required hardware features) verified by caller + tests (riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L50, gemv_residual_cubecl.rs L205-226) |
| `predicate-gated-skip-empty-workset-step` | whole-step elision on empty workset | A stochastic sampler can draw zero masked positions — the naive path runs the full forward/loss/backward/optimizer sequence anyway and the count-based loss normalizer divides by zero unless a LATE guard inside the loss fn catches it → one host-side integer check at the TOP of the step elides the entire dispatch batch AND guarantees n_masked >= 1 at the normalization site (div-by-zero guarded by construction; composes with fuse-count-into-mutation-pass whose fused count IS the predicate; riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L851-853 + L1243-1245 + L1546-1548, the late-guard-only contrast at L485-490) |
| `share-pipeline-set-across-subsystems` | Arc-shared pipeline set | Each subsystem (loss, backward, optimizer) builds its own pipeline set — every create_*_pipeline re-validates + re-compiles the same WGSL, once per consumer → build ONE set at orchestrator setup, Arc-share clones into every constructor + dispatch site: compile paid once per training run, per-step cost a ref-count bump (the cross-subsystem analog of prebuilt-bind-groups which pre-builds per dispatch site; riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L1092 + L1134-1148 + L1278 + L1433/L1459-1475) |

### Category E: Parallelism patterns (13 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `multi-dim-grid-launch` | multi-dimensional parallelism | 1D nested loop → 3D parallel grid (batch×head×time) |
| `block-count-clamp` | grid dimension clamping | Unbounded blocks → clamped to 65535 |
| `backward-as-conjugate` | backward kernel reuse | Separate backward → forward with conjugate flag |
| `reverse-scan-via-double-flip` | reverse-direction scan | Sequential reverse loop → flip+cumsum+flip (reuses forward scan) |
| `pairwise-distance-decay-matrix` | decay matrix materialization | Sequential per-step decay → N×N matrix from arange distance |
| `softmax-topk-renormalize-scatter` | `MoE` sparse gate | Dense gate → softmax+topk+renorm+scatter sparse gate matrix |
| `split-reduction-scalar-prepass` | reduction-split for fusion | CPU reduction + upload full tensor → scalar pre-pass (O(batch) output) + fold into main consumer (riir-gpu compute_inv_rms) |
| `single-plane-sufficient-reduction` | redundant-plane elimination | N planes all computing identical sum → one plane (8× wasted FLOPs, riir-gpu Issue 611 fix) |
| `broadcast-uniform-skip-reduce` | thread-uniform broadcast, not reduce | Block-reducing a value that is ALREADY identical in every thread (derived entirely from already-reduced broadcasts) sums the same value blockDim.x times — WRONG result, not just wasted barriers → broadcast instead: thread 0 writes smem, one barrier, every thread reads (riir-gpu cudarc_kernels/backward.rs L492-501 — the documented T7.2 validation catch where softmax-backward z came out 256× too large; distinct from single-plane-sufficient-reduction which is dispatch-level plane redundancy) |
| `workgroup-equals-plane-native-reduction` | plane-sized workgroup geometry | Workgroup sized to the data width (128 threads) forces every full reduction to bridge plane boundaries through a strided smem tree (up to 3.2× bandwidth cost, ~2304 barriers/head; a shuffle reduction scoped smaller than the workgroup is also a silent-wrong-answer hazard — Issue 610: plane_sum caught 1/4 of the dot) → size the workgroup to EXACTLY one hardware plane (32 threads) + register-block width/plane elements per lane: the native shuffle covers the FULL logical reduction and broadcasts for free, zero threadgroup memory, zero barriers (riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L256-262 + L356-363 + L650-653) |
| `smem-broadcast-scalar-then-parallel-scatter` | post-reduction broadcast scatter | After a workgroup reduction, the one thread holding the result must not also perform the entire dependent scatter (width serial global writes, all other threads idle — the single biggest bottleneck in the original DeltaNet forward port, Issue 616) → the leader publishes the scalar through the already-reduced slot, ONE barrier, then EVERY thread writes its own column of the dependent update: 1 global write per thread (riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L165-180) |
| `epilogue-must-be-workgroup-local` | ownership-scoped fusion legality | An epilogue may only consume data at the granularity the executing workgroup OWNS — no cross-workgroup sync exists to order it after a foreign write: plane-per-row GEMV epilogues may read per-row scalars (residual[row], scale[row]); a norm fusion requires the whole row reduction inside ONE workgroup; ops needing cross-row/cross-workgroup data (attention over KV) cannot be epilogued — attention stays 9 dispatches while post-attn drops to 1 and MLP to 4 (fusion legality is an ownership question, not just dependency; riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L49 + L371-375, gemv_residual_cubecl.rs L50-55, norm_residual_cubecl.rs L57-59) |
| `counter-derived-seed-stateless-noise` | stateless per-iteration RNG seeding | One shared RNG stream threaded through the training loop — replaying step k requires replaying 0..k, skipped steps perturb later streams → derive each iteration's seed from its loop counters packed into disjoint bit fields (((step as u64) << 32) | epoch): every iteration's noise is a pure function of its indices (standalone replay, skip-independence) and the same seed crosses to the GPU as ONE uniform with the thread index mixed in-kernel — bit-identical to the noise_corrupt.wgsl kernel; inline wrapping-mul/add LCG with >> 33 high-bit extraction (the statistically good half; riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L828-842 + L1526-1537 + L39) |

### Category F: Flash-attention tiling (10 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `online-softmax-streaming` | online softmax | 2-pass max+exp → streaming m/acc single pass |
| `exp2-log2-base2` | base-2 numerics | exp/log → exp2/log2 (faster on hardware) |
| `block-pointer-2d` | 2D block pointer | Manual offsets → block_ptr 2D tile load |
| `heuristic-compile-dispatch` | heuristic dispatch | Runtime branch → compile-time autotune heuristic |
| `causal-diag-offdiag-split` | causal mask split | Diagonal+off-diagonal → separate kernels (no mask check) |
| `shared-mem-aware-tile` | shared-memory tile sizing | Fixed tile → occupancy-aware (load vs compute bound) |
| `kv-block-streaming` | KV block streaming | Load all KV → stream one block at a time |
| `strict-causal-mask-tril-offset` | strictly-causal mask | tril(0) → tril(-1) (exclude diagonal) + NaN guard |
| `block-local-reference-frame-for-overflow` | overflow-safe decay | Global prefix diff (overflows fp32) → per-block reference frame (bounded) |
| `two-stage-mask-softmax-then-conv` | two-stage mask | Mask after conv only → -inf before softmax + 0 after conv |

### Category G: Linear-attention recurrence (19 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `state-forget-gate` | gated recurrence | Ungated write → `state *= exp(g); state += k⊗v` |
| `householder-rank1-update` | Householder reflection | Naive write → project out stored component then write |
| `chunkwise-parallel-recurrence` | chunked parallelism | Sequential recurrence → chunk-level parallel + inter-chunk reduction |
| `recurrent-vs-chunk-dispatch` | backend dispatch | Single path → recurrent (decode) vs chunk (train) |
| `multi-write-per-step` | multi-write recurrence | Single write → N writes per step (DeltaNet) |
| `chained-two-pass-recurrence` | two-pass recurrence | Single pass → two chained gated passes with softmax bridge |
| `per-key-dim-diagonal-gate` | per-dim decay gate | Per-head scalar gate → per-key-dim vector gate |
| `erase-replace-low-rank-transition` | diagonal+low-rank transition | 2-phase forget+write → 4-phase erase+decay+replace+write (RWKV-7) |
| `oja-subtractive-normalization` | Oja normalization | Write k⊗v → project out stored component `k − Sv` first |
| `identity-plus-low-rank-delta` | I+ba^T rotation | Forget gate → `(I+ba^T)` additive-rotational update |
| `cumsum-gate-to-decay-matrix` | pairwise decay from cumsum | Per-step gate loop → cumsum + pairwise diff + exp |
| `gate-then-correct-delta-rule` | gated delta rule | Correction against un-decayed state → decay first then correct |
| `gate-then-multi-householder-write` | gated multi-Householder | Gate per inner write → one gate + N inner delta writes |
| `online-gram-ridge-solve` | online ridge regression | Batch solve → online gated Gram accumulators + per-step solve |
| `momentum-gated-memory-recurrence` | SGD momentum + EMA memory | Plain GD → coupled momentum + EMA memory recurrences |
| `current-token-bonus-read-before-update` | boosted read + plain write | Write-then-read → boosted read (transient bonus) then update |
| `wkv-logsumexp-three-channel-state` | 3-channel logsumexp WKV | 2-channel EMA (overflow) → 3-channel (num/den/max) |
| `row-owned-register-blocked-recurrence` | state-matrix row ownership | One workgroup owns the whole per-head state (every row's dot needs a barriered tree; every algebra step re-touches global memory: 4 read + 2 write passes over S per step) → rows are INDEPENDENT within one decode step: one workgroup per (head, row), the row register-blocked into d/plane strided columns per lane, all five steps (decay, retrieve, delta, update, readout) on registers between ONE global read and ONE write — ~3× cut of the memory-bound gating quantity + 32× occupancy headroom (n_head → n_head × head_dim cubes; riir-gpu deltanet_cubecl.rs L255-294, Issue 619; distinct from chunkwise/recurrent-vs-chunk (sequence-axis) — this is state-matrix parallelism within one step) |
| `reverse-running-decay-weight` | dematerialized decay matrix | Materialize the pairwise intra-chunk decay matrix inter_decay[t,j] = Π α (O(C) multiplies per pair or a cumsum+exp pass) + a consume pass → the consumer loop itself runs j from t DOWNWARD carrying ONE running scalar: start at 1 (empty product), consume, multiply α[j] after use — O(C) per token, zero intermediate memory; the downward direction anchors the empty product (riir-gpu deltanet_chunked_cubecl.rs L640-712; the exact inverse of cumsum-gate-to-decay-matrix / pairwise-distance-decay-matrix) |

The patterns are Rust-shaped pseudocode so the shared `RustAnalyzerPruner` can
validate them in the fast path. When the full validator lands (CubeCL compile +
numeric compare), the patterns will carry real kernel source.

## Router — `KernelExpertRouter`

Wraps `katgpt_core::variable_rank_domain_expert::pick_domain<15, 5>` (Plan 558):

```rust
pub const NUM_KERNEL_EXPERTS: usize = 15; // one per corpus category (A–O)
pub const ACTIVITY_DIM: usize = 8;        // M, N, K, dtype, hw, is_attention, is_fusion, is_ir_transform

pub fn route(&self, activity: &[f32; ACTIVITY_DIM]) -> usize {
    pick_domain::<NUM_KERNEL_EXPERTS, ACTIVITY_DIM>(activity, &self.directions)
}
```

**Plan 003 (2026-08-14):** router wired to the corpus (310 rules at Batch 40) via 15 category
experts. Each expert owns its category's `rule_ids` via `KERNEL_CATEGORIES`.
- **Activity vector** = raw physical (kernel signature: `[M, N, K, dtype, hw,
  is_attention, is_fusion, is_ir_transform]` — 8-dim per Plan 004, normalized
  to `[0, 1]`). The `build_activity()` helper is the consumer-facing constructor.
- **Domain directions** = latent (hand-crafted category centroids — see
  `CATEGORY_KERNEL_DIRECTIONS` const; mining via `CommittedFieldBlend` deferred).
- **Expert index** = raw categorical (synced).
- Modelless argmax — zero allocation.

**Corpus wiring accessors:**
- `category_for_expert(idx) -> KernelCategory` — maps expert → category.
- `rules_for_expert(idx) -> &[&str]` — maps expert → category's rule_ids.
- `expert_for_rule(rule_id) -> Option<usize>` — reverse lookup.

**Plan 004 (2026-08-14):** extended the activity vector from 5 to 8 dims by
adding 3 operation-class one-hot axes (`is_attention`, `is_fusion`,
`is_ir_transform`). **All 15/15 categories are now reachable** (was 13/15 in
Plan 003 — categories C and O were unreachable due to 5-dim rank deficiency).
The `build_activity()` helper provides the consumer-facing constructor for
constructing activity vectors from shape + operation-class flags.

The `kernel_opt` feature enables `katgpt-core/variable_rank_domain_expert`
so `pick_domain` is reachable.

**Composition example** (`examples/kernel_opt_demo.rs`): the router is
composed with the drafter + corpus end-to-end. The demo routes 8 representative
kernel signatures, narrows each to its category's rules (~4–10 vs 150 flat — a
15× candidate reduction), then drafts + validates a fix. This is the reference
composition for future kernel_opt consumers. Run with:
`cargo run --example kernel_opt_demo --features kernel_opt`.

## Thermal — `KernelThermalRouter` + `ThermalTier`

The FlashAR/QGF thermal-tier pattern: Plasma→Hot→Warm→Cold→Freeze escalation.

| Tier | What runs | Where | Latency | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Plasma | Modelless cache hit (Stage 1 drafter) | M3 Metal (local) | µs | ✅ shipped |
| Hot | Stage 2 adaptive drafter (Issue 010) | M3 Metal (local) | µs | ✅ shipped |
| Warm | LoRA draft + validate | 4090 CUDA | ms | ⏳ deferred (riir-train) |
| Cold | Full validate vs reference | 4090 CUDA | s | ⏳ deferred |
| Freeze | Archive snapshot | cold storage | offline | ⏳ deferred |

**Scaffold behavior:** returns `ThermalTier::Plasma` for ALL calls — the
modelless path is the primary tier per the modelless-first mandate. The
escalation logic (Plasma → Hot on cache miss, Hot → Warm on Stage 2 pool miss,
Warm → Cold on LoRA uncertainty, Cold → Freeze on archive trigger) lands when
the 4090 path is wired (riir-train).

`ThermalTier` is `#[repr(u8)]` (1 byte, sync-efficient):

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Default)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum ThermalTier {
    #[default]
    Plasma = 0,  // modelless cache
    Hot = 1,     // Stage 2 adaptive
    Warm = 2,    // LoRA draft — DEFERRED
    Cold = 3,    // full validate — DEFERRED
    Freeze = 4,  // archive — DEFERRED
}
```

## Verifier — `KernelVerifier` (compile-only stub)

Behind `kernel_verify`. **STUB** — delegates to the shared `RustAnalyzerPruner`
for syntax validation. Full CubeCL compile + numeric compare vs reference is
deferred (Proposal 012 §Phase 0). `is_full_validator_available()` returns
`false` in the stub so callers can detect the deferred state.

## Domain classification (per AGENTS.md)

- Kernel **shapes** (M, N, K, dtype, hw) = raw physical (deterministic,
  replayable — needed for quorum sync of kernel selection decisions).
- Expert **directions** = latent (dot-projection onto learned centroids,
  projected to a scalar expert index via `pick_domain`). NOT synced — only
  the resulting expert index crosses the sync boundary.
- Thermal tier = raw categorical (1 byte, `#[repr(u8)]`).

### Category F: Flash-attention tiling (Batch 2, 8 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `online-softmax-streaming` | flash-attention online softmax | Full matrix materialization → streaming running max+sum+rescale |
| `exp2-log2-base2` | base-2 math | Natural exp/ln → base-2 exp2/log2 (dedicated GPU instruction) |
| `block-pointer-2d` | structured 2D block pointer loads | Manual offset arithmetic → structured block ptr with boundary check |
| `heuristic-compile-dispatch` | compile-time heuristic dispatch | Runtime branch → compile-time specialization (dead branch elimination) |
| `causal-diag-offdiag-split` | causal matrix diagonal/off-diagonal splitting | Uniform precision → diagonal fp32 + off-diagonal bf16-safe |
| `shared-mem-aware-tile` | shared-memory-aware tile sizing | Fixed tile → SM-aware (Hopper larger, Ampere smaller) |
| `kv-block-streaming` | KV block streaming through shared memory | Full KV load → block streaming (O(block) shared mem) |
| `strict-causal-mask-tril-offset` | strictly-causal mask via tril(-1) | Standard causal tril(0) → tril(-1) (exclude diagonal) + NaN guard |

### Category G: Linear-attention recurrence (Batch 3, 11 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `state-forget-gate` | exponential decay gate on recurrent state | Unbounded accumulation → `state *= gate.exp()` |
| `householder-rank1-update` | rank-1 Householder reflection | General update → error-correcting delta rule |
| `chunkwise-parallel-recurrence` | chunkwise parallelism for state-carrying loops | Fully sequential → chunk-parallel (intra-chunk matmul) |
| `recurrent-vs-chunk-dispatch` | sequence-length-aware backend dispatch | Always chunk → recurrent for short, chunk for long |
| `multi-write-per-step` | multiple state writes per timestep | Single write → N Householder writes under one gate (DeltaProduct) |
| `chained-two-pass-recurrence` | chained sequential recurrences with softmax bridge | Single pass → K-state then V-state with softmax bridge (GSA) |
| `per-key-dim-diagonal-gate` | per-dimension diagonal decay gate | Scalar decay → per-key-dim vector gate (gla) |
| `erase-replace-low-rank-transition` | diagonal+low-rank state transition | 2-phase update → 4-phase erase/decay/replace/write (rwkv7) |
| `oja-subtractive-normalization` | Oja subtractive normalization | Raw write → project-out-then-write `β(k − Sv)` (gated_oja_rule) |
| `identity-plus-low-rank-delta` | (I+ba^T) state rotation | Decay-gated update → additive-rotational (iplr_delta_rule) |
| `cumsum-gate-to-decay-matrix` | pairwise decay from per-step gate | Per-step gate → cumsum+diff pairwise matrix (simple_gla) |

### Category H: MoE dispatch + backward pass (Batch 4, 9 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `grouped-launch-cumsum-offsets` | grouped GEMM with prefix-sum offsets | N separate launches → single grid with cumsum-derived group offsets |
| `l2-grouped-tile-schedule` | swizzled L2-cache-aware tile mapping | Row-major block map → grouped swizzle for L2 reuse |
| `fused-matmul-activation` | pointwise activation in matmul epilogue | Separate matmul+activation passes → fused epilogue |
| `fused-backward-chain` | two backward ops fused into one kernel | Two backward kernels with global handoff → one fused kernel |
| `activation-recompute-budget` | partial activation save with in-kernel recompute | Save all activations → save fraction + recompute rest (checkpointing) |
| `atomic-scatter-reduction` | lock-free atomic scatter for parallel writes | Locked scatter → relaxed atomic add |
| `weight-grad-batched-outer-product` | weight gradients as batched outer product GEMM | Per-token rank-1 updates → batched tiled GEMM |
| `csr-index-via-prefix-sum` | GPU-side CSR sparse layout via prefix sum | Host nonzero+sort → in-kernel prefix sum + atomic counter |
| `autotune-aggregate-workload-key` | autotune keyed on aggregate workload size | Fixed config → autotune on total work (Msum) for irregular batches |

### Category I: NSA block-sparse selection (Batch 5, 4 rules)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `block-sparse-gather` | block-sparse indexed gather via selection array | Dense iteration over all N → index-array block gather |
| `precompute-scalar-reduction` | hoist invariant row reduction out of per-block loop | Recompute delta per block → precompute once in separate pass |
| `block-compression-before-routing` | pool to coarse blocks, route on compressed reps | Score all fine positions → pool to blocks, score compressed, attend top-k |
| `sliding-window-plus-sparse` | decompose attention into local + global sparse, gated sum | Single attention pass → local window + global sparse with gates |

### Category J: Varlen packing + chunked reductions (5 rules — 3 Batch 6 + 2 Batch 10)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `varlen-packing` | pack variable-length sequences via cu_seqlens, no padding | Padded batch (waste on padding) → packed 1D buffer with cumsum offsets |
| `chunk-reset-cumsum` | cumsum that resets at chunk boundaries | Full cumsum → cumsum reset every chunk_size elements |
| `cumsum-additive-bias` | additive bias via cumulative sum of a gate | O(N²) bias matrix → O(N) cumsum difference |
| `chunk-split-mean-pool` | chunked reduction via split + per-chunk mean | Running sum accumulator → split(C).mean() exposes fixed chunk size |
| `ragged-chunk-pad` | pad ragged tail to block_size | Branch on last-chunk → pad tail, one uniform path |

### Category K: Autotune dispatch (Batch 7, 4 rules — burn-cubecl)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `panic-safe-autotune-candidate` | panic-resilient autotune candidate | Direct call (panics on bad shape) → catch_unwind → Err (autotune skips) |
| `multi-priority-tune-groups` | shape-keyed priority-ranked autotune groups | Flat candidate list → priority-ranked groups (MAX/HIGH/MIN/NEVER) keyed on shape |
| `shape-kind-dispatch` | matmul-kind-keyed kernel family dispatch | Single general GEMM for all shapes → GEMV for matvec, GEMM for general |
| `vector-width-pow2-sweep` | vectorization width as an autotune dimension | Fixed vector width → sweep C ∈ {1,2,4,8,16,32,64} as autotune candidates |

### Category L: Conv-as-GEMM + memory layout (Batch 7, 6 rules — burn-cubecl)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `conv-1x1-as-matmul` | 1×1 convolution as direct matmul (skip im2col) | im2col expansion + GEMM → reshape to 2D + matmul (no column expansion) |
| `contiguity-guard-before-reshape` | stride-contiguity guard skips unnecessary relayout copy | Always-copy-into-contiguous → check stride contiguity, skip copy if already compatible |
| `cube-count-bounded-batch-pack` | grid-limit-aware batch packing | One batch per launch → pack max batches ≤ cube-count limit (find largest divisor) |
| `stride-align-vectorization-key` | stride-byte-alignment determines vectorization width | Fixed vectorization → trailing-zeros of (stride × bytes) picks vector width |
| `anchor-shape-bucketing` | shape bucketing for autotune cache reuse | Exact-shape autotune key → bucketed key (nearest N) so similar shapes share cache |
| `carry-dual-layout-dead-slot` | parameterized consumer-layout carry | A separate conversion kernel/pass between a producer's compact output and the consumer's native layout (prefill's [ch, ks-1] carry → decode's [ch, ks] conv_state) → parameterize the producer's write stride + index offset (two scalars): the SAME kernel writes either layout directly; the conv_state variant leaves slot 0 stale — provably safe because the decode shift overwrites it before any read (riir-gpu deltanet_chunked_cubecl.rs L77-98) |

### Category M: Kernel parametric polymorphism (9 rules — 3 Batch 8 + 2 Batch 10 + 1 Batch 29 + 1 Batch 30 + 2 Batch 31)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `op-family-type-dispatch` | parametric kernel polymorphism over op trait | N copy-pasted kernels (one char differs) → one kernel body + `OpFamily` trait with N associated-type impls |
| `inplace-output-alias` | output buffer reuse via mutability check (skip allocation) | Fresh output alloc every call → `can_mut` check aliases output to disposable input (zero alloc) |
| `layout-permute-for-coalescing` | memory layout permutation for coalesced channel access | NCHW strided channel access → permute to NHWC (channel innermost, coalesced), permute back |
| `reshape-rank-for-backend` | rank adapter — reshape to backend-required rank | Leak rank contract to caller → reshape (1,-1,h), run kernel, reshape back |
| `detached-broadcast-insert` | non-differentiable broadcast via detach + view | Materialize full broadcast → detach() + insert dim (view, no alloc) |
| `enum-dispatch-separate-kernel-per-variant` | DSL-limitation variant specialization | Generic kernel parameterized over epilogue behavior is impossible in DSLs without kernel generics (and pays a runtime branch per element where it compiles) → enumerate the fusion-combination space as a host-level #[repr(u8)] enum, compile ONE specialized kernel fn per variant (zero in-kernel branching, each with exactly its own Array count — visible even between dtype twins with byte-identical launchers), drive buffer plumbing from enum metadata methods (extra_input_count, needs_residual, total_array_count); the enum organizes dispatch, the kernel list stays flat (riir-gpu epilogue/mod.rs L47 + L99-103 + L137-154, gemv_residual_cubecl.rs L76 + L140) |
| `zero-init-side-branch-frozen-base-path` | adapter-identity mode collapse | Running a FROZEN un-adapted model (distillation teacher) beside a trainable adapted student = a SECOND no-adapter forward path or a merged-out weight copy (duplicate code + duplicate device weights) → initialize the adapter's B matrix to zero (standard LoRA init: A=random, B=zero) and run the SAME adapter-capable forward for both roles: out = W·x + B·(A·x) = W·x exactly, by construction — one code path, one uploaded weight set, the identity is mathematical, not maintained by discipline (riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L1044 + L1097-1114, constructor contract lora/mod.rs L160) |

### Category N: dtype + alignment specialization (13 rules — 2 Batch 8 + 4 Batch 9 + 2 Batch 10 + 1 Batch 15 + 1 Batch 16 + 1 Batch 20 + 1 Batch 30 + 1 Batch 31)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `dtype-specialized-float-kind` | dtype-keyed intrinsic dispatch for low-precision floats | Generic powf (absent/imprecise for f16/bf16) → match on `FloatKind`, cast to concrete dtype, call its intrinsic |
| `alignment-gated-zero-copy-view` | alignment-gated zero-copy slice view via handle offset | Always-copy slice → if byte offsets are alignment-aligned, return a VIEW (offset-only metadata, zero copy) |
| `int2-pack-weights` | pack 4 ternary weights per byte | 1 weight/byte → 4 weights/byte (2-bit encoding, 4× bandwidth) |
| `per-token-activation-quant` | per-token symmetric int8 fake quant | Per-element quant → per-token (straight-through gradient) |
| `ternary-weight-quant` | per-tensor ternary {-1,0,+1} fake quant | Per-element quant → per-tensor ternary (BitNet b1.58) |
| `upcast-before-loss` | upcast to f32 before log/exp in loss | fp16 loss (log underflows) → f32 upcast before log/exp |
| `batched-upcast-map` | batched type promotion via map over tuple | Per-tensor upcast calls → `map(upcast, tuple)` (signals independence) |
| `dtype-restricted-backend` | backend restricted to precision-stable dtypes | All-dtype backend → dispatch on dtype (fast path only for stable) |
| `water-fill-tail-dim-shared-codebook` | non-uniform bit allocation + tail-shared codebook | Uniform per-dim codebooks at max_bits → top d_eff dims get per-dim codebooks, tail dims share one b_low-bit codebook (riir-gpu spectralquant/dequant, 2.3× codebook memory reduction) |
| `norm-decoupled-low-bit-scoring` | norm-decoupled low-bit key quantization | Store full-magnitude key, quantize directly (needs 8 bits) → store per-position L2 norm separately as a scalar, quantize the unit-normalized key at 2-4 bits, reconstruct score as norm × dot(unit_q, unit_k_quantized) (riir-gpu forward_turboquant, 4× less key memory) |
| `base-3-trit-packing-5-per-byte` | base-3 mixed-radix ternary packing (5/byte) | 2-bit code packing (4 ternary/byte, 2.125 bits/weight) → base-3 trit packing (5 ternary/byte, 1.75 bits/weight — 17.6% denser; decode via byte % 3, byte /= 3 which compiler optimizes to mul-by-inverse) (riir-gpu gemv_ternary_cubecl trit path, Issue 628 T2; REGIME-DEPENDENT — wins on Metal where div/mod is cheap + bandwidth-bound, loses 2.1× on CUDA scalar ALU per Issue 607 + incompatible with __dp4a int8 path) |
| `pad-uniform-pod-to-min-binding-size` | explicit 16-byte uniform pad + test pin | Param structs sized by their fields alone — an incidental 12-byte struct fails the var<uniform> min-binding-size class and each kernel hand-rolls its own buffer → pad with explicit _pad u32 fields to exactly 4×u32 = 16 bytes so ONE fixed-size (size: 16) buffer helper serves every kernel, and pin the layout with size_of tests (== 16, % 16 == 0): adding a field without re-padding breaks CI instead of failing on the GPU at runtime (zero runtime cost — the padding is dead bytes; riir-gpu dllm/mod.rs L33-60 + L74-82, dllm/tests.rs L30-49) |

### Category O: IR strength reduction (Batch 8, 4 rules — cubecl-opt)

| Rule ID | GPU concept | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| `strength-reduce-mul-pow2` | IR strength reduction: multiply by power of 2 → left shift | `x * 16` (mul unit) → `x << 4` (shift unit, frees throughput-limited mul) |
| `strength-reduce-mul-pow2-plus-minus-one` | IR strength reduction: multiply by (2^k ± 1) → shift ± self | `x * 15` (mul) → `(x << 4) - x` (shift + sub, 2 instructions, no mul unit) |
| `strength-reduce-div-pow2` | IR strength reduction: divide by power of 2 → right shift | `x / 8` (slow div unit, tens of cycles) → `x >> 3` (1 cycle, exact for unsigned) |
| `strength-reduce-mod-pow2` | IR strength reduction: modulo by power of 2 → bitwise and mask | `x % 64` (div/mod unit) → `x & 63` (bitwise-and unit, exact for unsigned) |

Batch 206 (Plan 206, 2026-10-07 — the 10-07 staged kernel slot): 2 rules, corpus 769 → 771 (D +2; entries_gpu_76.rs) — **the llama.cpp upstream pair** (sources `ggml-org/llama.cpp` @ `ab09ea4c148868aa4919b36e86962bf6ff2f32a7` #29442 + @ `51ce9c11a6f2dfa895696c0048c4333e8953b728` #28782, both MIT, both merged 10-06; the two leads graded subject-read at the 10-07 watch pass, full quote verification done AT this batch per the standing law; ALL quotes re-verified VERBATIM at the pins, line numbers read from `git show <sha>:<path>` at the post-commit files — NOT hunk-derived; `.raw/b206-llamacpp` depth-150 clone removed at close). D +2: `widening-conversion-row-chunk-staging-budget` (#29442 — a dtype-WIDENING staging conversion feeding a library GEMM is bounded by ROW-CHUNKING against a fixed byte budget (`max_src0_convert_size = 512ull * 1024 * 1024`; admission two-conjunct: staging exceeds budget AND `rows_per_chunk < src0->ne[1]`; chunks re-enter the SAME impl as shallow-header row slices — the guard lives in the outer wrapper, no recursion) AND the chunked writes derive the lead dimension from the BYTE STRIDE (`ldc = nb1 / sizeof(float)` replacing ne0 at all five cuBLAS call sites, strideC `ne1*ldc`) — the comment 'F32 chunks can have padding between rows to preserve the original destination stride' states why the two halves are ONE story: chunking REQUIRES stride-respect, and the ldc derivation is what widens the dst admission assert from bare contiguity to `ggml_is_contiguous_1(dst)`; distinct-from'd vs row-independent-pass-block-bounded-scratch (fixed block PARTIALS, not budget-derived conversion slices), chunk-encoder-submits-below-driver-limit (submission limit, not staging budget), stream-invariant-operand-skips-staging-pipeline (skips staging vs bounds it), narrow-m-l1-panel-sizing (L1 K-extent vs staging bytes), and ids-path-rebinds-stride-meaning (the stride-CONFUSION hazard face vs this rule's deliberate stride-DERIVATION widening face)) + `per-thread-stream-blocking-op-explicit-sync` (#28782 — a blocking device op on the legacy default stream implicitly serializes against every stream device-wide AND races concurrent host threads on the one shared default stream; the fix is stream CHOICE with the completion contract kept: `cudaMemsetAsync(..., cudaStreamPerThread)` + `cudaStreamSynchronize(cudaStreamPerThread)` replacing the bare blocking `cudaMemset` in buffer-init's padding path; the CI half — the removed `# TODO: fix multi-threaded for ROCm` carve-out, subject 'ci : re-enable test-backend-ops -j for ROCm' — is the hazard evidence: multi-threaded init was running the default-stream race; context law: the file's other ~10 memset entry points already ran the per-thread pair at the parent — buffer-init was the LAST blocking holdout; distinct-from'd vs captured-graph-init-unconditional-and-stream-ordered (capture node-set + visibility edge vs stream choice), producer-store-evict-last-pins-cross-stream-consumer (cache policy vs stream assignment), and rust_perf's lone-stream-graph-replay-mode-switch (population-keyed mode vs per-op stream)). Both rules NotBenchable-annotated (no in-source numbers — the decision content is the admission/scheduling law). Dedup: zero prior owners under both vocabularies (chunk-vocab rules are submission limits / work-stealing floors / block-bounded partials; no blocking-default-stream rule existed). Batch 207 (Plan 207 / Research 242, 2026-10-07 — the 10-07 staged kernel slot, the cubecl pair): 2 rules, corpus 771 → 773 (B +1, D +1; entries_gpu_77.rs) — **the cubecl leads** (sources `tracel-ai/cubecl` @ `9f6bb84fb8da51cae518f756e6adb598de949dde` #1777 "Perf/minifloat decode" (3-part commit) + @ `04a894b2a9922de56df4b17a07cca335cd4165e1` #1751 "Fix/metal profile window drains", both MIT OR Apache-2.0, both merged 10-06/10-07; staged at the 10-07 grading row with first-read grades, full quote verification done AT this batch per the standing law — ALL quotes re-verified VERBATIM at the pins, line numbers from `git show <sha>:<path>` at the post-commit files; `.raw/b207-cubecl` removed at close). B +1: `minifloat-normal-code-shift-bias-diff-decode` (#1777 — a minifloat code's MAGNITUDE is already an f32's exponent-then-mantissa in order, so a normal code is ONE shift + ONE add of the bias difference pre-shifted into the mantissa field (`rebias = (F32_EXPONENT_BIAS - format.bias()) << F32_MANTISSA_BITS`), where the decode took the two fields apart and put them back; the subnormal arm chosen by MAGNITUDE alone (`magnitude < smallest_normal` — zero exponent leaves the magnitude as the mantissa alone, the one place the layouts disagree), the sign ONE or at the end (`result | sign`), and the carry-safety clause: the largest exponent, rebiased, still fits the field; every code decodes exactly — exhaustive e4m3/e5m2 tests pass and FAIL when the bias is off by one (the off-by-one test IS the admission guard, the enumerable-domain-bit-identity family's instance); the ~3%-of-NVFP4-gemv figure is M2 Pro source-reported, provenance never a bar; the PLACED-decode arm: `e2m1_words_to_f16_placed` decodes each e2m1 code as f16 placed `E2M1_F16_LIFT` (2^14) short of its value, exactly, for the caller that multiplies by a block scale anyway — fold the lift into that factor ONCE rather than pay a multiply a value (the lifted fn is the placed fn times the lift); bit-PLACEMENT + offset-folding is the decode family's third mechanism (B194 owns the replicated-LUT GATHER, the B199 rider the parity+field-arithmetic tables, ue8m0 the scale ENCODING); the EMULATED-CONVERSION ADMISSION arm: `Types::emulated_conversion` + `Features::conversion_is_emulated` — a NEW admission-predicate member beside B176's shared-admission family (Metal registers its fp8/fp4; a kernel decoding such a type itself pays no more than the conversion and may fold what the emulation spends into work it already does); distinct-from'd ×6 incl. dtype-native-source-read (kernel-path exact widening vs the admission-predicate member — composing not merging) and quantized-stored-bytes (the storage-form axis this decode arithmetic operates ON)) + D +1: `profile-window-drains-queued-work-before-open` (#1751 — the measurement-honesty D-class member: Metal samples a pass's begin when its ENCODER STARTS and orders an encoder after earlier work only through a buffer the two share, so a window opened behind work it shares nothing with timed that work's tail and contention as the window's own — autotune queues exactly that work (a read past the caches) before every sample, and on an M2 Pro a decode gemv's samples read about three times their duration except where the read happened to finish first, and the tuner elected those outliers — corrupted measurement AND a wrong election derived from it; the fix names the backend fact ONCE (`TimestampAvailability` → `TimestampSampling`, InCommandStream vs AtStageBoundaries) with BOTH consequences hanging off it: a resolve waits for completion (as before) and a window DRAINS the queue before it opens (new — flush + `device.poll(wait_indefinitely)`, the system-timing sync's analog); only the stage-boundary backend drains — Vulkan writes the begin at the bottom of the pipe behind every earlier command and keeps the cheap flush; the new macOS-only regression test queues the nothing-shared read and asserts the window times its own work; kinship: fused-serving-stack-measured-negative-ledger (the negative-ledger sibling — fix the window, don't just record the negative), seed-warm-the-scored-seam (opposite polarity of the same hygiene), construction-identity-row-classes-replace-probe (correctness axis vs this timing axis), fallthrough-toggle-oracle (work OUTSIDE the timed region vs a toggled-off path inside it), hot-swap-weights-preserve-autotune-caches (autotune caches vs autotune measurement)). Both rules NotBenchable-annotated (all figures M2 Pro source-reported).
